Wild blow first three-goal lead at home since 2019 in loss to Avalanche

📅 Nov 28, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The Minnesota Wild didn't have a great Thanksgiving week in St. Paul. A shutout loss to the Avalanche on Friday night marks their first losing streak at home all season. The way it happened makes it even more frustrating.

Minnesota remains without superstar defenseman Quinn Hughes, who is currently on IR with an upper-body injury. That didn't stop the Wild from taking a commanding 3-0 lead into the third period behind two goals from Joel Eriksson Ek and one from Blake Coleman.

Things fell apart for the Wild in the third period. Devon Toews was the first Avalanche player to find the back of the net with 6:23 to go. Artturi Lehkonen scored one minute later, and T.J. Hughes tied the game with less than one minute left to force overtime.

Neither team scored in overtime, which forced a shootout. Minnesota lost the shootout and moved to 0-7 in shootouts this season. The disappointing result was also the first time the Wild blew a three-goal lead at home since 2019.

Minnesota is now 18-4-6 on the season. The Wild are off Saturday night before hitting the road to face Chicago at 1 p.m. CT on Sunday.

4 steps Thunder need to take in order to snap three-game losing skid

📅 Nov 28, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

4 steps Thunder need to take in order to snap three-game losing skid appeared first on ClutchPoints. Add ClutchPoints as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

Amid head coach Mark Daigneault and the Oklahoma City Thunder's three-game losing streak, All-Star Shai Gilgeous-Alexander remained optimistic about the future, and for good reason. Jalen Williams, Cason Wallace, and Chet Holmgren sustained long-term injuries that sent the Thunder's season into a tailspin. Oklahoma City (10-9) has fallen to eleventh in the Western Conference standings. However, the beginning wasn't so bad.

The Thunder beat the Memphis Grizzlies, the Washington Wizards, and the Cleveland Cavs twice. Short-term success for a team missing three starters, but the injuries didn't stop there. The Thunder also lost Jaylin Williams and Aday Mara to recent injuries in seven of their last nine games. With a thin frontcourt, Oklahoma City will have to reinvent itself once more.

The Thunder's razor-thin margin of error is a common theme, but if Oklahoma City is to snap its three-game losing skid, here are four steps that should secure its first win in four tries.

Watch sports LIVE with fuboTV (free trial)

While Alex Caruso has been a reliable spark off the Thunder's bench lately, starting him sets an ideal tone on both ends of the floor. Caruso finished with 11 points, four blocks, two assists, two rebounds, and two steals in the Thunder's 113-106 loss to the Timberwolves.

When the Thunder's offense goes stale, Caruso's defensive stops help Oklahoma City find its rhythm. Adding Caruso to the starting lineup would also help the Thunder spread the floor. He's connected on 44.4% of his 3-point attempts this season. Opposing defenses can't leave him open.

Caruso starting should lead to higher-percentage looks for Shai Gilgeous-Alexander and Ajay Mitchell, both of whom have attacked the rim more than any other Thunder player this season. Maximizing Gilgeous-Alexander and Mitchell's production is most important while the Thunder find ways to put wins together without three starters and only one center in Isaiah Hartenstein.

Amid the Thunder's devastating injuries, Ajay Mitchell is averaging a career-high 17.2 points per game this season. He's taken on the role of the second-leading scorer alongside Thunder All-Star Shai Gilgeous-Alexander. However, opposing defenses have found ways to limit his production, as he's now in scouting reports.

Now, it's up to the Thunder head coach Mark Daigneault to put Mitchell in position to earn more frequent trips to the free-throw line. Oklahoma City is 3-0 whenever Mitchell finishes a game with six or more free-throw attempts, including a 30-point performance in a 125-114 win against the Phoenix Suns.

Mitchell's offense is critical for the Thunder's success without its core players. And while Jalen Williams and Cason Wallace won't be returning any time soon, unlocking the best version of Mitchell between now and Chet Holmgren's return puts Oklahoma City in position to win games. Holmgren sustained a right knee injury that will keep him sidelined until late December.

Thunder rookie Bennett Stirtz scored a season-high 21 points in a 123-113 loss to the Miami Heat. The confident shooter connected on nine threes in two games, following an 18-point performance with a career-high. Stirtz is taking on a role he hadn't anticipated at the start of the regular season, but he's answered the call anyways.

Earning Thunder head coach Mark Daigneault's trust, Stirtz earned his first career start in Wednesday's loss to the Timberwolves. He'll continue to receive the green light as a reliable shooter who can create defensive stops on the opposite end of the floor. Perhaps starting every now and then, depending on the opponent.

Stirtz will only improve through the reps as a rookie. And while the Thunder's three consecutive losses aren't ideal, giving the rookie guard extended runs and experience is best for the team amidst an extended list of injuries.

Thunder head coach Mark Daigneault sees growth in his team's approach despite their three-game skid. However, Oklahoma City has struggled to defend the 3-point arc. The Timberwolves connected on 17 threes in Wednesday's loss. And when the Thunder find themselves on the wrong end of a lopsided run, the threes come raining down quickly.

While the margin for error remains thin, Daigneault hopes the Thunder can find consistency in their approach night in and night out despite their shortcomings.

"The common theme is: it's a 48-minute game, and we need to execute a few more plays in a game that comes down to one or two possessions in order to be on the other side of the result," Daigneault said. "And that can happen in the second quarter. It can happen in the first quarter, and it can happen late. So, we just got to fight for those margins right now. Improve on the things that we know the game is going to come down to. There's plenty of controllable things that we can handle in these games.

"And that shows up in the first quarter, shows up in the fourth quarter; shows up throughout. We just got to continue to invest in those things. Trust that process and allow the wind to turn for us a little bit."

With two road games next up against the Milwaukee Bucks and the Brooklyn Nets this week, the Thunder will look to return to the win column with a 10-9 record on Thanksgiving.

Mike Macdonald on Sam Darnold, when healthy: "I think he's done a good job"

📅 2026-11-28  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Quarterback Sam Darnold is back as Seattle’s starter this week, after missing two games due to quad and rib injuries. It’s an opportunity to improve on what has been a challenging start to the 2026 season.

On Friday, coach Mike Macdonald was asked to assess Darnold’s performance when healthy.

“I think he’s done a good job,” Macdonald said. “As a football team, we’ve been good enough to go 6-4, and we want to get better. Yeah, he’s right there with everybody else.”

Well, he’s not right there with the player who replaced him. Drew Lock has started three games and played all but one drive of another. His record is 4-0. Darnold is 2-4.

And their numbers are starkly different. Darnold has thrown 10 touchdown passes and nine interceptions. Lock has thrown seven touchdown passes and no interceptions.

Lock’s passer rating is 125.6. Darnold’s is 79.5.

Lock’s games have come against the Patriots, the Cardinals twice, and the Raiders. Darnold has faced the Commanders, Chargers, 49ers, Broncos, Chiefs, and Bears — a much tougher slate of opponents.

The Seahawks won a Super Bowl with Darnold. That counts for something. Still, they’re 6-4. They have an opportunity to get a seventh win on Sunday, with Brock Purdy out for the game against the 49ers.

Mike McCarthy just 'focused on winning' after Aaron Rodgers saga

📅 Nov 28, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Mike McCarthy helped guide the Pittsburgh Steelers to a resounding 27-5 victory over the Denver Broncos on "Black Friday" after one of the franchise's most tumultuous weeks in recent memory.

The trouble began after the Steelers announced veteran quarterback Aaron Rodgers was being benched in favor of Mason Rudolph at 11:10 p.m. ET on Tuesday, Nov. 24. The following day, Rodgers did not report to the Steelers' facility and instead aired his grievances with McCarthy in an appearance on "The Pat McAfee Show" before Pittsburgh released the 42-year-old quarterback.

The accusations claimed McCarthy had fallen asleep in team meetings and had performed a "1, 2, 3, Packers!" during a halftime huddle breakdown with his team, among other issues. Naturally, it garnered significant attention in the NFL world, with many decrying Rodgers for breaking the sanctity of his team's locker-room bond.

And one of the most notable reactions came from McCarthy's predecessor, Mike Tomlin, who referred to the organization as having a "dumpster fire" on its hands following Rodgers' release.

Following the Steelers' Week 12 win, McCarthy was asked for his reaction to those comments.

"I really don't have one," he replied, stoically, during his postgame news conference. "Let's just be real here. There's only so much time and energy in the day. I think you have to, when you go through things like that, I have to put all my energy into winning."

"I get that this is part of the responsibility and the opinions and comments of others is why I'm in here to answer questions," McCarthy added. "But I'm focused on winning."

McCarthy's approach paid off, as the Steelers were able to improve to 7-4 with their victory over the Broncos. That has put them a half-a-game behind the Cincinnati Bengals in the AFC North race while giving them more breathing room in the wild-card race, as they now are a game-and-a-half ahead of the No. 8 seed New England Patriots.

McCarthy appreciated earning that victory after what he acknowledged was a difficult week for his team.

"It was definitely a hard travel week for us as a football team," he said. "I'm proud of our players, I'm proud of our coaching staff, I'm proud of our support staff and I really appreciate all the other support."

The Steelers were playing at home at Acrisure Stadium in Pittsburgh in Week 12, so it wasn't immediately clear whether McCarthy was using the phrase "hard travel week" metaphorically as a reference to a series of events the team endured amid the Rodgers saga.

Either way, McCarthy came out of the win pleased with his team. He praised Rudolph for being able to effectively guide the team without Rodgers and also praised his veteran-laden defense for its stalwart performance against Bo Nix.

But above all else, McCarthy was grateful for the team's veteran leadership for establishing "urgency" after a meeting they held earlier in the week.

"I think it says a tremendous amount about the team," McCarthy said, referencing the win. "I really probably want to pivot off the leadership council meeting. Everything that was discussed was healthy. A lot of things in there, but what came out of it was urgency. And I thought our leaders did a tremendous job of bringing the urgency to their rooms and it definitely showed up."

"You definitely saw the urgency today," McCarthy later added. "That's what really stood out for me as far as the vibe in the locker room. Our guys do a good job of that. From the get-go, our leaders really had the guys going. And our defense got us started. We played the game that we needed to play. Just very, very impressed and very proud of the way we handled this."

This article originally appeared on USA TODAY: Mike McCarthy just 'focused on winning' after Aaron Rodgers saga

“I’m not a very intelligent person”: Lennart Karl’s astonishing statement

📅 Nov 28, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Great hope of Bayern Munich, Lennart Karl is having a mixed start to the season despite 3 goals and 2 assists in 15 matches. Often a substitute, the 18-year-old attacking midfielder continues to learn. The German international (5 caps) spoke in a meeting with Bavarian fans in South Tyrol and admitted that he would not have known what to do with his life outside of football in a rather surprising way.

“I didn’t have a plan B. I still wouldn’t know what to do. I’m not a very intelligent person. I have to be honest. For me, there was only one plan. My parents told me to finish my studies. I refused. I got up every morning with only one desire: to play football.“, he declared in comments collected by Sky Germany.

Former NBA lottery pick Rob Dillingham gets punched by Wizards rookie Felix Okpara during G League game

📅 Nov 28, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Playing for the G League Westchester Knicks, former NBA first-round pick Rob Dillingham was involved in a scuffle during Friday's matchup with the Capital City Go-Go.

In the fourth quarter of Westchester's 125-104 win, Dillingham was punched by Capital City center Felix Okpara at the top of the 3-point line. Dillingham pushed Okpara on his way down the court, and the center looked at the official for a foul call.

When one wasn't made, Okpara clotheslined Dillingham across the neck and shoulders, knocking him to the floor.

Both teams then gathered in a scrum past the Westchester basket, going off the court as Okpara backpedaled toward the baseline. Coaches, officials and arena security personnel then separated each group of players.

Ermal Kuqo, the Knicks' senior director of minor league operations, and his 6-foot-10, 270-pound frame also got involved, which surely helped provide a significant obstacle to fisticuffs.

Dillingham and Okpara were subsequently both ejected from the game. Dillingham finished with 10 points and 8 assists, while Okpara scored 12 points with 9 rebounds for the Go-Go.

Okpara was the Washington Wizards' second-round pick in the 2026 NBA Draft, selected No. 46 overall out of Tennessee. In three games with the Wizards, he averaged one point and 0.7 rebounds in five minutes of play. In the G League, Okpara is scoring 11.4 points, 8.8 rebounds and 2.4 blocks per game.

Dillingham was the San Antonio Spurs' 2024 first-round pick, selected No. 8 overall out of Kentucky. He was traded to the Minnesota Timberwolves on draft night for a first-round pick swap in 2030 and first-rounder in 2031.

Last season, the T-Wolves dealt him midway through his second season in a trade involving Ayo Dosunmu going to the Chicago Bulls. Before training camp this season, he was moved to the Charlotte Hornets in exchange for Buddy Hield but was subsequently waived. In November, he signed with the New York Knicks' G League team.

In three games with Westchester, Dillingham is averaging 21 points and 6.3 assists per game while shooting 35% on 17 3-point attempts.

Aaron Rodgers claims he was 'barred' from Steelers' facility: Why QB went scorched earth on Mike McCarthy

📅 Nov 28, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

Based on how they played Friday, it seems like the Pittsburgh Steelers have already put the Aaron Rodgers drama behind them, but the same can't be said for the four-time NFL MVP.

Rodgers has taken some heat this week for breaking the sanctity of the locker room and he decided to respond to that accusation by sending a text message to Al Michaels, who was on the call for Pittsburgh's Black Friday game against the Broncos.

During the fourth quarter of the Amazon Prime stream, Michaels read out loud what Rodgers had sent him.

"A lot of talk about the sanctity of the locker room," Michaels said of what Rodgers wrote. "The code or sanctity exists to protect the integrity of the locker room, not to provide immunity from accountability for its leadership. They took this public. Never heard from the head coach. Then they leaked at 11 p.m. They also barred me from the facility. That's why I went to [Pat] McAfee."

It's a short text, but there's a lot to unpack here.

For one, let's start with the fact Rodgers claims he was "barred'' from the team facility. The Steelers announced Rodgers' benching at 11:10 p.m. ET on Tuesday and they did that even though no one in the organization had actually spoken to Rodgers before the announcement came out (They did speak with his agent, though).

Steelers coach Mike McCarthy made the decision to bench Rodgers after the QB gave him an ultimatum at some point on Tuesday: relinquish play-calling duties or release him. Although Rodgers didn't get released at that point, that did lead to his benching.

The QB didn't show up for practice on Wednesday, and according to multiple reports, that was because the team had excused him from practice, but according to Rodgers, it was because he was banned from the facility.

The Steelers' decision to lock Rodgers out is why he ultimately chose to talk to Pat McAfee on Wednesday. The interview was a wild ride, with Rodgers throwing out multiple accusations at Mike McCarthy, including that he had missed meetings, fallen asleep during meetings and skipped walk-throughs.

Just hours after the interview with McAfee ended, Rodgers was released.

During Thursday's slate of Thanksgiving games, Rodgers was called out by Tom Brady for breaking the "sanctity" of the NFL locker room, and an accusation like that from Brady likely rubbed Rodgers the wrong way, which would explain why he used Brady's wording in his text to Michaels.

Based on Rodgers' claim, he may not have done the McAfee interview if he hadn't been barred from the facility, and if there had been no interview, this situation might have turned out differently. But it also seems Rodgers was determined to burn every bridge on his way out of town, so it's hard to say whether things would have turned out differently.

Either way, the Steelers have moved on from the former quarterback and they did so in dominant fashion by beating the Denver Broncos 27-5 on Friday.

Dillon Brooks gets ejected with 23 seconds left vs. Rockets after getting into it with Kevin Durant

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Phoenix Suns guard Dillon Brooks and Houston Rockets forward Kevin Durant got at the end of Houston's 116-104 win on Wednesday night.

Durant was chippy all night, making sure to deliver extra trash talk against his former team.

With less than 30 seconds left in the game, the Rockets led by 10. In a wild moment, Durant and Brooks weren't really playing; they were just talking to each other near halfcourt while the action continued.

Houston's Fred VanVleet hit a jumper with 23.8 seconds left to make it a 114-102 game in the Rockets' favor. Brooks and Durant were chirping at each other and got in each other's faces.

The referees assessed each player a technical foul. Brooks had received a technical foul earlier in the quarter with 4:32 left in the game. VanVleet drained a three and motioned to Brooks; they chirped and were both assessed technicals.

Durant finished the game with a double-double: 20 points, 10 rebounds. With 46.3 seconds left in the second quarter, the veteran forward drained a 3-pointer. That basket helped him become the fifth player in NBA history to score at least 33,000 points. He joins LeBron James (43,672 and counting), Kareem Abdul-Jabbar (38,387), Karl Malone (36,298) and Kobe Bryant (33,643).

The win moved Houston to 14-5. The Rockets return to action Saturday, hosting the Philadelphia 76ers.

The loss dropped Phoenix to 9-8 on the year. The Suns had won four straight before Wednesday's loss, and now turn their attention to taking on the Dallas Mavericks on Friday night.

Plainville stuns Farmington to complete first 10-0 football season in school history

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

PLAINVILLE - On Thanksgiving eve 2026, Plainville, Connecticut was the little town that didn't sleep.

The lights of Dinty Stadium were blazing and unlikely to be shut down any time soon as the Plainville Blue Devils and their fans were madly celebrating the first 10-0 season in the 101 years this town has been playing high school football after a dramatic 33-28 victory over Farmington in the 15th playing of the Olde Canal Cup.

Down by two, just stopped on fourth down at the 1-yard line with time running out, the Blue Devils were facing their first loss of the year. Instead, improbably, miraculously, Farmington fumbled.

Plainville junior defensive lineman Nick Serrano recovered and, two plays later, senior quarterback Anthony Abarno lofted an 8-yard touchdown pass to sophomore wide receiver Christian Hairston in the middle of the end zone with 34 seconds to play.

When the Plainville defense thwarted a hook-and-ladder play and Farmington couldn't get off one last play, the Blue Devils could, indeed, start spreading the news. They had gone unbeaten in 1946, but that was 8-0. This was 10-0, with a home quarterfinal as the No. 2 seed in Class SS dead ahead next Tuesday. It's Plainville's first trip to the CIAC football playoffs since 2019.

Farmington, in need of a win to qualify in Class L for the first time since 2013, fell to 6-4 and can only hope that all the 6-3 teams playing Thanksgiving Day go down to defeat to have any hope of sneaking in. If not, the River Hawks will have to be content with a strong season that was their first over .500 since going 6-4 in 2018.

With just over two minutes to play, it looked like Farmington's season would extend and Plainville's perfection would end. Famrington defended an inside run on the fourth down at the 1. Needing to just run out the clock, the River Hawks fumbled on third down.

Plainville took over at the 2 with 1:14 to go, lost six yards on the first snap. On second down at the 8, Abarno took the shotgun snap, rolled a few steps to his right and put a pass into the end zone that Hairston brought down between two defenders.

It was Abarno's fifth touchdown pass of the night and his second to Hairston. The senior QB, among the state's top passers this season, also threw two scoring strikes to Billy Putnam and one to Chase Bounchard.

The second to Putnam, a 2-yarder that Putnam cashed in reaching the ball across the goal line, came with 4:46 left to play and pulled the Blue Devils to within 28-26. Farmington denied the two-point pass attempt to stay in the lead.

Down 20-14 at halftime, Farmington grabbed the upper hand during a dominant third quarter. The River Hawks opened the second half with a 14-play, 61-yard drive capped by a 12-yard pass from junior QB Mason Kiniry to senior receiver Grant Kintner. Tyler McMahon's PAT put Farmington ahead 21-20.

After initially failing to extend that lead on a missed 22-yard field goal, Farmington got the ball back on a fumble recovery by senior linebacker Bretton Phillips and this time cashed in on Kiniry's fourth-and-14 pass from the 24 to Chris Huelin just over two minutes into the fourth quarter.

Junior running back Tommy Zadrozny had Farmington's two touchdowns in the first half on runs of 11 and 12 yards. The first score came after Farmington pooched the opening kickoff and recovered at the Plainville 37.

The Blue Devils countered with Abarno TD passes to Bouchard, Putnam and Hairston. Sophomore defensive lineman Aaron Madore recovered a fumble for Plainville and also chalked up a handful of hurries on Kiniry.

Farmington, which had won the three previous Olde Canal Cup games, still leads the series 10-5.

Dylan Feltt. Playing with a soft cast on one hand and with a sprained meniscus that was cramping on him frequently, Plainville's senior inside linebacker embodied his team's gritty, never-surrender mind-set that produced Wednesday night's victory.

Packers rumors: Could Aaron Rodgers return to Green Bay? Insider reveals QB ‘dislikes’ GM Brian Gutekunst

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Packers rumors: Could Aaron Rodgers return to Green Bay? Insider reveals QB 'dislikes' GM Brian Gutekunst appeared first on ClutchPoints. Add ClutchPoints as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

Aaron Rodgers is no longer expected to return to the Pittsburgh Steelers. Within just one day, the Steelers went from making the veteran quarterback inactive for their upcoming game to releasing him. With speculation surrounding Rodgers' future in the NFL now becoming a major talking point, one NFL insider has revealed why a reunion with his former team, the Green Bay Packers, is unlikely.

Matt Schneidman of The Athletic reported that Rodgers has no interest in returning to Green Bay because of his strained relationship with general manager Brian Gutekunst.

"Forgot to mention this, but Aaron Rodgers straight up told me after the trade that he dislikes Brian Gutekunst," Schneidman wrote.

Watch sports LIVE with fuboTV (free trial)

A return to Green Bay would be an emotional homecoming for Rodgers, who spent nearly two decades with the franchise and became the face of the organization.

The 42-year-old quarterback began his NFL career with the Packers after being selected in the first round of the 2005 NFL Draft. He spent his few three seasons behind Brett Favre before taking over as the starting quarterback.

Soon after becoming the starter, Rodgers established himself as one of the greatest quarterbacks in franchise history. He led Green Bay to a Super Bowl victory, earned four NFL MVP awards and became one of the most accomplished players in the league before his departure.

A reunion would give Rodgers an opportunity to return to the organization where he achieved some of the greatest accomplishments of his career. However, his previous departure from the franchise was complicated enough to make the possibility of a reunion difficult to imagine.

The Packers eventually moved forward with Jordan Love as their starting quarterback. With Love now established as the team's quarterback, Green Bay has little reason to revisit its decision unless circumstances change significantly.

Undoubtedly, Rodgers remains one of the most accomplished quarterbacks in NFL history. However, the dramatic end to his Steelers tenure has suddenly left Rodgers' NFL future uncertain. After being benched and subsequently released, the veteran quarterback now faces questions about whether he will continue playing or bring his legendary career to a close.

LdC: PSG-Rome classified as high risk

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

After the international break, Paris Saint-Germain will return to the Champions League with the reception of AS Roma at the Parc des Princes on November 25. A meeting placed at very high risk according to the French authorities, as indicated RMC Sport. If, usually, the visitor parking lot at the PSG grounds can accommodate between 2,000 and 2,300 supporters depending on the matches, the capacity of this area of ​​the Parc des Princes will be reduced to 1,500 spectators for the Italians, by order of the Paris Police Prefecture and the National Division for the Fight against Hooliganism (DNLH).

This measure is explained in particular by the close proximity between certain fervent supporters of Paris Saint-Germain and those of Napoli. This attachment could lead to a rise in tension around this meeting with the AS Roma ultras. UEFA must also rule on this situation, which requires 5% of the stadium to be reserved for visiting fans.

2026 NFL Thanksgiving schedule: What to know about Thanksgiving and Black Friday games

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

The NFL has cooked up something special for this year's Thanksgiving. For the first time, a Thanksgiving Eve game was added to the Thanksgiving week slate. It's essentially an appetizer for Thursday's triple-header feast, with the dessert coming Friday with the Black Friday game.

Each game has storylines big enough to fill any plate. To keep up with everything, we've provided a rundown of how to follow the games in real time. We've also included the major thing to know for each matchup.

What to know: While he's had a lot of success in Chicago, Ben Johnson is 0-2 against Detroit, his former team. Johnson's former boss, Dan Campbell, has won four straight games against Chicago, seven of his last eight in the series and has a 7-3 overall record against the Bears.

Coaches aside, this is a matchup between two of the NFL's hottest teams. The Bears have won five straight games, while the Lions enter Thursday with a six-game winning streak. The winner of this game will take sole possession of first place in the NFC North.

It'll be especially interesting when the Lions' fifth-ranked offense is on the field against Chicago's sixth-ranked defense. Jahmyr Gibbs, who is one touchdown away from breaking Barry Sanders' NFL record for the most touchdowns by a player before his 25th birthday, should have his moments. He's facing a run defense that is just 25th in the NFL in average yards-per-carry allowed.

What to know: Dallas is a sizable favorite, but there are several reasons to believe that the Eagles are ripe for an upset.

The Cowboys were 10-point favorites when they were upset by then-winless Tampa Bay Buccaneers back in Week 5. Dallas lost that game to a backup quarterback (rookie Jalon Daniels), and they'll face another backup Thursday in Joe Milton III, who used to play for America's Team. Milton just won his first career start Sunday against the Steelers. He made several clutch plays, including his third-down conversion to DeVonta Smith that iced the game.

If Dallas wins on Thursday, it will be the first time it's won back-to-back games under Brian Schottenheimer. The Cowboys' inability to string together consecutive wins is yet another reason why Philadelphia could successfully play the role of spoiler.

What to know: This game will be billed as the latest chapter in the Josh Allen-Patrick Mahomes rivalry. Overall, the Allen-Mahomes series is tied up at five wins apiece, which makes Thursday night's game a rubber match. Allen is 0-4 against Mahomes-led teams in the playoffs, but he has a 5-1 record in regular-season starts against Mahomes that includes five straight victories.

Both quarterbacks will look to put on a show, but the winner of this game will likely come down to which supporting cast outperforms the other. On paper, the Chiefs have a few advantages, most notably their second-ranked scoring defense that will look to exploit Allen's Kryptonite: turning the ball over. Allen has thrown nine interceptions this year, including five in his last four games.

It'll be interesting to see how the Chiefs' young players fare in a big-time environment, especially Emmett Johnson, Kansas City's rookie running back, who has tallied consecutive 100-yard rushing performances while filling in for injured starter Kenneth Walker III.

What to know: This game is pretty simple. If the Steelers are going to pull off an upset, they're going to have to take advantage of Denver's suspect run defense and third-down offense. If they can't do either of those things, the Broncos will more than likely hand them their third straight loss.

Jaylen Warren was having a monster season for Pittsburgh until he and the rest of the Steelers' offense hit a wall over the last two weeks. Pittsburgh's lack of depth at receiver has led defenses to crowd the line of scrimmage, leaving little running room for Warren and back mate Rico Dowdle.

A positive for Pittsburgh is the emergence of wideout Max Hurleman, who caught a team-high six passes for 61 yards and a score against the Eagles. Plus, the recent return of Roman Wilson and the possible return of Michael Pittman, who hasn't played since Week 4, are bright spots. The Steelers will also have DK Metcalf after the league decided not to suspend him following his ejection during Sunday's loss in Philadelphia.

Nets topple Bucks in NBA Cup clash to snap five-game skid

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The teams with the two worst records in the Eastern Conference faced each other Wednesday night in Milwaukee, but no one needed to accuse either the Nets or the Bucks of tanking in this one.

Neither team can win the draft lottery after the season because both franchises essentially traded their respective 2027 first-round picks years ago, but the Nets at least halted their five-game losing streak with a defensively improved 104-97 victory in an NBA Cup game at Fiserv Forum.

Julius Randle netted a team-high 28 points as the Nets (4-13) posted their first NBA Cup victory after losses earlier in the season against the Pistons and the Magic.

Michael Porter Jr. was held to 12 points on 4-for-14 shooting with 11 rebounds to follow another quiet scoring night (11) Monday in Minnesota.

Julius Randle, who scored a team-high points, shoots a floater during the Nets' 104-97 win over the Bucks on Nov. 25, 2026, in Milwaukee. Jeff Hanisch-Imagn Images

Egor Dëmin had an active game with 14 points and a plus-15 rating in 25 minutes off the bench.

Noah Clowney nailed three 3-pointers among his 17 points, and Day'Ron Sharpe contributed 11 points, 14 boards and five assists for the Nets.

Tyler Herro, who had a career-best 50-point game Saturday against the 76ers, scored 21 of his 28 points in the second half for the Bucks (2-17).

"We've lost a few in a row, so we've gotta try to get a win no matter who we're playing against," Porter told the YES Network before the game. "They're an NBA team. They are at the bottom of the standings right now, but so are we, so it's gonna be a dogfight, and we gotta try to get out of here with a win."

The NBA instituted rule changes in the offseason to discourage tanking for draft position, essentially penalizing the teams with the worst three records in the league with lower odds to secure the No. 1 overall pick in the lottery.

The Nets' 2027 first-round pick is controlled by the Rockets from an unprotected pick swap as part of Brooklyn's deal to acquire James Harden in January 2021.

The Bucks, who dealt away superstar forward Giannis Antetokounmpo to the Heat over the summer, also had committed to a pick swap involving their 2027 first-rounder when they acquired guard Jrue Holiday from New Orleans in 2020.

The Nets had allowed at least 70 points in the first half four times during their five-game skid, but this slow start was more due to offensive inefficiency.

Noah Clowney, who scored 17 points off the bench, puts up a shot during the second quarter of the Nets' road win over the Bucks. Jeff Hanisch-Imagn Images

Brooklyn missed five of its first six shots and spotted the Bucks a 9-2 lead in the first three minutes.

But Porter knocked down two straight 3s and scored eight as part of a 15-1 run to vault the Nets into an eight-point advantage en route to a 28-24 edge through 12 minutes.

A 3-pointer by Clowney and a jumper by Randle extended the cushion to 10 early in the second. The Nets led by as many as 13 before the Bucks closed within 51-47 before intermission.

"We have to close quarters better," Randle, who had 16 in the half, told YES.

Rookie point guard Mikel Brown Jr. scored all six of his points in the second half after posting 17 in his first NBA start Monday in Minnesota.

Dëmin came off the bench again and contributed five points before halftime, including a driving bucket through the lane in the closing minute.

Herro only managed seven points in the first half, but his 3-pointer pulled the Bucks even, 53-53, early in the third quarter.

Day'Ron Sharpe, who scored 11 points and grabbed 14 rebounds, slams home a dunk during the Nets' win over the Bucks in Milwaukee. Jeff Hanisch-Imagn Images

Brown finally recorded his first two buckets after seven misses to push Brooklyn back ahead, and two late dunks by Sharpe and a traditional three-point play by Dëmin helped the Nets carry a 77-71 edge into the final quarter.

Keon Ellis (plus-14) and Grant Nelson provided some defensive energy off the bench to open the fourth, and Porter converted an and-1 to stretch the lead back to 11 with 10:38 remaining.

Moritz Wagner didn't play in the second half due to what the Nets said was left knee soreness.

Chivu: ‘Inter had right attitude, cannot dominate start to finish in Champions League’

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Cristian Chivu insists Inter had the right attitude in their 2-0 win over VfB Stuttgart, warning ‘in the Champions League you cannot dominate from start to finish.’

The Nerazzurri were feeling the pressure after fumbling a 4-2 lead to draw 4-4 with Atalanta in Serie A at the weekend, and knew this was a must-win game to leapfrog VfB Stuttgart in the Champions League table.

It was a tough match, decided only by the Petar Sucic penalty for handball, and Lautaro Martinez coming off the bench to lock down the result for 2-0.

“We relied on mentality, organisation, intensity, accepting that Champions League games are not easy, that you cannot dominate them from start to finish,” Chivu told Sky Sport Italia.

“At times you need to stay in the match, try to make the most of opposition errors, but also try to be proactive when possible. The moments of a match are important and you need to read them.

“We did well not to get swayed by their intensity in the first 20 minutes, so we accepted that we had to defend a bit deeper, but still with energy and pressing them on the ball. We did well to defend the crosses, as we knew they would be dangerous down the wings.

“It is also true that we created chances in the first half and with a little more sharpness or determination, we could’ve taken the lead before going into the break. We stayed in there, and were eventually rewarded with the victory.”

This result means Inter are feeling more solid in the Champions League after three consecutive victories, having started the campaign with back-to-back defeats against Real Madrid and Club Brugge.

There are still concerns that the Nerazzurri lose intensity at times and need to be woken up before reacting.

“The job of a coach is to transmit the right motivation in every match. It’s not always easy to maintain the same level, sometimes we do it better than others, but the important thing is that the team remains united and nobody starts pointing the finger at anyone else,” added Chivu.

“We must always try to do better in every competition. We cannot always be perfect, but what we must never lack is the right attitude and mentality. We saw that tonight, and it is no coincidence that we brought home the victory.”

Did Chivu have to give the squad an intervention after they collapsed from 4-2 to draw 4-4 with Atalanta on Sunday?

“There always need to be interventions, when things go well or badly. We made a clear analysis of that match, because for around 75-80 minutes we played well, we lacked something in the final 10 minutes, but that doesn’t mean throwing away all the good things that were done that night.

“We are trying to keep a clear head in analysing the situation and whittling down those errors that can be tactical, technical or mental,” replied the Inter coach.

There was bad news too, as Alessandro Bastoni limped off with a thigh injury after 40 minutes, replaced by Federico Dimarco, moving Carlos Augusto to central defence.

Unpacking every crazy moment from Sporting-Man United

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

Mauricio Pochettino was denied a first win in an incident-packed night

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Three penalties, two disallowed goals, one fluke, and a “main-character energy” referee: Manchester United’s 2-2 draw with Sporting in the Champions League had a bit of everything.

Mauricio Pochettino was denied his first win as United manager as his side saw a late winner disallowed by VAR in a bonkers clash in Lisbon.

Referee Szymon Marciniak was in the thick of the action throughout and in the 84th minute ruled that Patrick Dorgu had fouled Sporting goalkeeper Kaique Pereira, who let a corner from Bruno Fernandes slip through his fingers.

But that was not all - here’s a breakdown of all the main incidents from a crazy night.

After a subdued start in Lisbon, Mauricio Pochettino saw his new defence implode as they conceded two penalties in the space of three minutes.

The hosts received the chance to open the scoring when Lisandro Martinez was ruled to have fouled winger Geny Catamo by catching him with an outstretched leg. If it appeared soft, justice was perhaps served when Senne Lammens saved a tame effort from Sporting striker Luis Suarez low down to his left.

But in farcical scenes, Lammens then conceded another penalty just 75 seconds later when he crashed into Maximiliano Araujo in the box. Suarez took the second penalty and made no mistake to give Sporting the lead, as United conceded the opening goal for the seventh match in a row.

It was the first time United had conceded two penalties in the same Champions League match since 1998, while they have now conceded five in total in all competitions this season. And Lammens went from hero to zero as he undid his penalty save by quickly conceding another.

Referee Szymon Marciniak ruled that Youri Tielemans had committed a foul on Goncalo Inacio when he slid to poke the ball through to Amad Diallo, who headed into an empty net at the second attempt after the goalkeeper deflected his shot onto the crossbar.

However, because Marciniak had already blown his whistle to stop play following the challenge from Tielemans, VAR could not intervene. Tielemans did not appear to commit a foul and instead won the ball fairly against Inacio when he went to ground to play the ball through to Amad.

If Marciniak hadn’t blown for the foul, the VAR would have been able to allow it, if they had ruled that Tielemans had not fouled Inacio. “It was a dreadful decision,” former United midfielder Owen Hargreaves said on TNT Sports. “I don’t know what [the referee] saw. [Tielemans] played the ball. It wasn’t reckless.”

The United captain and former Sporting player had already equalised when he converted from the spot, after Lisandro Martinez turned into a muarading left winger to win the third penalty of the night, but his second of the night will perhaps go down as the most bizarre goal of the season.

The assist went to a divot in the pitch, as a low left-footed shot from Fernandes struck the turf and diverted away from the stranded goalkeeper Kaique Pereira to find the net. The uneven pitch effectively acted as a ramp as the ball suddenly jumped up and flew over Pereira, who was left with no chance.

Just to add to the freakish nature of the goal, TNT Sports found that Fernandes appeared to have created the divot in the six-yard box the Sporting goal when he turned on the Estádio José Alvalade pitch, leading to his fortuitous bobble moments later.

Ivan Fresneda’s cross from the right wing went so high that it vanished from the frame of the TV screen, as the defender turned his back in disgust with how wasteful he had been. But when the ball eventually came down, Lammens presented Sporting with a gift.

The goalkeeper dropped the ball at the feet of Luis Suarez, who hooked in his second of the match acrobatically to draw Sporting level. It was another error from the increasingly fragile Lammens, his second of the match and just a few weeks after his costly spill against Aston Villa.

Patrick Dorgu, Lisandro Martinez and Leny Yoro were all in close proximity as Kaique Pereira failed to punch clear an inswinging corner from Bruno Fernandes, with referee Marciniak ruling that Dorgu had jumped into the goalkeeper.

Pereira flapped at the cross as the ball slipped through his fingers, but Dorgu was ruled to have “committed a foul on the goalkeeper before the ball entered the goal” and Marciniak ruled it out after being sent to the pitchside monitor by VAR.

Earlier this month, United saw a very similar goal stand when Senne Lammens challenged Aston Villa goalkeeper Zion Suzuki and Dorgu scored to earn a 4-4 draw in the Premier League, but the KMI Panel later ruled that it was correctly awarded.

This was salt in the wounds for United, as they received their fifth red card of the season, and fourth from their last five matches. Tielemans, of course, will have argued that he should not have received a yellow card in the first half, if Amad’s equaliser had been allowed to stand.

This time, Tielemans was late as he slid in on Sergi Altimira, although the Sporting midfielder certainly made the most out of what appeared to be minimal contact. Tielemans will now be suspended from the home match against RB Leipzig next month.

&quot;Now isn't the time&quot; - Kompany avoids finger pointing as Bayern suffer first defeat

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

On the 18th attempt, Bayern Munich suffered their first defeat of the campaign on Wednesday, with Lille securing a 2-1 win in the Champions League.

Making the trip to northern France, Vincent Kompany's depleted side took the lead via Dayot Upamecano in the 15th minute, and strolled into half-time carrying a deserved 1-0 lead.

But, the hosts would flip the clash on its head in the second half, with Ayase Ueda's equaliser followed by an own-goal from Aleksandar Pavlovic just minutes later, condemning Der Rekordmeister to their first defeat since April.

Speaking to DAZN after the final whistle, boss Kompany reflected on what was most definitely a tale of two halves.

"The first half was pretty much how we'd envisioned our game would go," said the Belgian, after his side strutted their stuff with relative ease despite operating with a depleted 11.

"It highlighted both our strengths and our weaknesses. But overall, it was a good first half.

"In the second half, we just couldn't find our rhythm anymore, And then, of course, you don't have that breathing room, that space where you're so far ahead that the game is already decided. That's part of football. Of course, you have to play the game until the very end.

"We tried. So, that's that, there's no blame to be placed on anyone, but the second goal, of course, shouldn't have happened," he added, referencing Pavlovic's own goal in which the midfielder will not want to see again when analysing the defeat.

However, the Bayern boss insisted that the blame game is far from the right path to take in this instance.

"From experience, I know these moments. I know that now isn't the time; we have to fight for our chances, stick together, fight for it and enjoy the battle; that's part of it."

With the club reviving the 'FC Hollywood' tag as a result of recent events, Kompany has the task of steadying the ship as the winter break approaches, and with a trip to Hamburg coming at the weekend, the German champions will be looking for a response to their first loss of the season, potentially easing the tension surrounding the club at this moment in time.

Bayern Munich stunned 2-1 by Lille in Champions League after second half collapse

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

It's all gone from bad to worse for Bayern Munich as the German Rekordmeister fell to a 2-1 loss to Lille in France in the Champions League. The result comes just a few short days after the disappointing 1-1 draw with FC Köln in the Bundesliga, a match in which Bayern lost both Harry Kane and Michael Olise to injury in the first half, with Kane facing a long spell on the sidelines.

Against Lille, Vincent Kompany started an attacking four of Lennart Karl, Tom Bischof, Nathaniel Brown, and Konrad Laimer. He really didn't have many other options with Harry Kane, Michael Olise, Serge Gnabry, Alphonso Davies, and Ismael Saibari all out injured, and both Jamal Musiala and Luis Díaz starting on the bench after returning from lengthy injury layoffs.

Bayern looked to be in control of the match even with an injury-hit squad, taking a 1-0 lead into halftime on a Dayot Upamecano header off a Bischof corner. Konrad Laimer missed a gilt-edged chance to make it 2-0 for the Bundesliga champions, leaving Bayern with a narrow lead at the break despite controlling 60% of possession and putting at least five shots on target.

In the second half, Lille dealt Bayern a one-two sucker punch in the 62nd and 65th minutes, scoring through Ayase Ueda and an unfortunate own goal from Aleksandar Pavlović as he tried to clear a shot that had come off the post. The midfielder falling to his knees and burying his face in his palms captured Bayern's frustration on a night when the club conceded twice in three minutes and left Lille empty-handed.

Speaking after the match, Laimer offered his assessment and said Bayern lost too much of the control and momentum it had in the first 45 minutes. "I thought we had a very decent first half, in which we implemented many of the things we had set out to do. I think we were very aggressive, had good energy, won a lot of duels, and picked up a lot of second balls. But we lost a bit of that in the second half. Then we conceded two goals, and things became far too easy for them. We also failed to produce the level of performance we are capable of. And now we're standing here with zero points," the Austrian said (via @iMiaSanMia).

Bayern now finds itself in 15th place in the Champions League table, needing results to climb back into the top eight for automatic knockout-stage qualification. With its next two matches in the competition coming against Slavia Prague and a struggling Manchester United squad, Bayern should have a chance to turn things around—though nothing is guaranteed. That's the nature of the Champions League, a point Laimer underscored. "That's Champions League football. That's how it is. Now it's simply about recovering quickly because we're going through a very intense period with very little time between games," he stressed.

How Does Gabe Vincent Signing Impact the Kings?

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

On Sunday, Nov. 22, the Sacramento Kings signed veteran point guard Gabe Vincent to a one-year, non-guaranteed deal. The move comes after it became increasingly apparent that Malik Monk was not cutting it as a backup point guard. The Kings have played Monk less and less as the season has progressed because their offense is poor with him on the floor.

Vincent slotting into the Kings' lineup as backup point guard shifts Monk to a secondary ball-handling role, where he has been more comfortable in the past. He also provides continuity behind rookie Darius Acuff Jr., a player the organization sees as their superstar of the future.

Acuff is third in the NBA in minutes per game, averaging 36.6. It is unprecedented for a rookie to lead that category, but the coaching staff has deemed it necessary to play him so much. With Acuff off the floor, turnovers increase, offensive possessions stall, and players take poor shots. Vincent represents stability; he makes wise decisions with the ball.

His numbers don't jump off the stat sheet — Vincent averaged just 3.9 points, 1.6 assists, and 1.1 rebounds in 13.3 minutes per game. What does stand out, however, is his minuscule 0.6 turnovers per game. The 1.6-to-0.6 assist-to-turnover ratio means he averages 2.67 assists for every singular turnover. Monk's ratio is 3.6-to-1.69, meaning he averages 2.11 assists before turning the ball over.

Vincent doesn't play himself off the floor; he does what the coaching staff asks of him in his minutes. One could categorize his play style as boring, but Kings fans would much prefer boring over unpredictable.

Looking beyond his offensive impact, Vincent is not a liability on defense. When engaged, he's a solid point-of-attack defender who plays with hustle and physicality. Monk is lack-luster defensively, so Vincent has a leg up on him there. Even if Vincent's shot isn't falling on a given night, he can ensure the second unit doesn't completely lose the game.

Vincent's journey to the NBA was anything but easy. After completing his college career at UC Santa Barbara, Vincent entered the NBA Draft, but went undrafted. He wound up playing for his hometown Stockton Kings in the NBA G League.

Vincent then worked his way to the NBA, receiving a two-way contract from the Miami Heat in 2020. He worked his way into the rotation and was a key piece for their 2023 NBA Finals run.

Whether Vincent is an All-Star or plays ten minutes per night, the advice he can give Acuff is invaluable. Acuff said it himself in a postgame press conference; he is not a "loser." He's not used to the trials and tribulations that come with leading a, frankly, terrible team. Vincent has experienced his fair share of bumps in his career, and he can help the young rookie navigate what this season has to offer.

Being a veteran presence in the locker room alone is important for the rookie. He hasn't played extended minutes yet, as he is easing into Doug Christie's system and getting back into game shape. He has the skill set to make an impact once the dust settles.

Adding Vincent leads to a level of competition to the team's backcourt. Monk may try to fight for his spot as the primary ball-handler in the second unit. He has already stepped up his game since Vincent's arrival.

Signing Vincent isn't necessarily a needle moving acquisition for a 2-15 Sacramento Kings team. It does, however, provide backcourt reassurance and takes a good deal of initiating off Acuff's plate. Acuff is tied for the fifth-highest usage rate in the league (31.6%). Passing off that burden to a veteran will go a long way to keeping the rookie healthy and confident.

Over a long, grueling 82-game season, a break from having your foot on the gas pedal is often needed. Signing Vincent is not just a solid move because of what he brings to the floor, but in what he symbolizes. He exemplifies continuity, sturdiness, and peace of mind.

Rams vs. Packers prediction: Can Green Bay continue success with deep ball? Will L.A. overcome injuries?

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

In the first-ever Thanksgiving Eve NFL game, the Los Angeles Rams play host to the Green Bay Packers. The Rams enter this game coming off their bye week, and they are incredibly banged up on offense. Puka Nacua is likely out for the year, as is Terrance Ferguson, after they each underwent surgery on lower-body injuries. Davante Adams remains out with his hamstring injury as well. The Rams will need their defense and their running backs to carry the day against a tough Packers team.

Green Bay is also coming off the bye, but is getting healthier for this game as Micah Parsons will make his season debut after tearing his ACL toward the tail end of last season. Parsons returning to the fold provides a significant boost to what has already been a good defense under Jonathan Gannon, and the offense remains one of the league's most explosive units with Jordan Love targeting Christian Watson and Matthew Golden down the field.

Will the Rams hold off the Packers at home, or will Green Bay pull off the upset? We'll find out soon enough. Before we break down the matchup, here's a look at how you can watch the game.

Green Bay's offense is entirely based on the passing game because the Packers simply cannot run the ball. Green Bay is last in the league at 3.1 yards per carry. The Packers' 670 rushing yards are 124 fewer than the next-closest team in the league (the Bengals). The Rams allow only 3.9 yards per carry and sport Myles Garrett and Aaron Donald up front.

The Packers are not going to find success on the ground here — and honestly, they might not even try to do so. They're probably just going to let Jordan Love drop back and let it rip. That's what they've largely done throughout this season, and it's worked quite well for them.

Love has completed 63% of his passes at 7.7 yards per attempt, with 20 touchdowns and seven interceptions. He's ninth in the league in EPA per play, according to Tru Media, thanks largely to the fact that he's created explosives at the fourth-highest rate in the league. When you have Christian Watson and Matthew Golden by your side, that's how the offense is likely going to be built. The Packers really lean into their strengths by having the strong-armed Love target their speedy wideouts with deep routes; Love has thrown 20-plus yards downfield at the second-highest rate in football.

The Rams have been the NFL's third-best defense against deep passes this year, according to Tru Media, with opponents completing only 8 of 30 deep passes for 257 yards, 1 touchdown and 1 interception. The 30 deep passes they've faced are the sixth-fewest in the league.

It'll be fascinating to see whether Green Bay continues to attack deep downfield or if Love, Watson, Golden and tight end Tucker Kraft instead work the short and intermediate areas of the field that the Rams encourage opposing offenses to throw to because of their coverage structures. It'll also be interesting to see if Trent McDuffie shadows one of the two wideouts or if the Rams play sides with him and Josh Wallace, who has taken the place of the injured Jaylen Watson.

L.A. has allowed the league's fewest catches and second-fewest yards to wide receivers this season, according to Pro-Football-Reference, with opposing quarterbacks targeting tight ends and running backs more often than they do against other teams. Tight ends have been targeted at the fourth-highest rate (27.4% of passes, according to Tru Media) against the Rams, while backs have been targeted at the second-highest rate (20.6%).

This could be a game where Kraft is more involved in the proceedings. The Packers don't throw to their backs very often because they are not good players, so they'll probably still force the ball to Watson and Golden, even if the Rams do limit wide-receiver production.

In their first game without all three of Nacua, Adams and Ferguson, the Rams used a highly unusual personnel rotation.

Their three leading skill-position players in snaps were all tight ends — and none of those tight ends was the one the Rams drafted in the second round. Their running backs shared the field for 18 snaps after having never previously played together in their careers, according to Tru Media. And one of their two primary wide receivers (rookie sixth-round pick CJ Daniels) played more than seven times as many snaps in Week 10 (44) as he had in his entire career before that (six).

The Rams were nevertheless able to rack up 436 yards, 28 first downs and 34 points against the decrepit Cardinals' defense. They ran the ball extremely well (202 yards) and were efficient in the passing game, with each of the wideouts creating an explosive play and the backs and tight ends chipping in with short and intermediate grabs. Matthew Stafford threw only 26 passes, but five of them created gains of at least 19 yards, and he averaged nearly 10 yards per attempt.

The Packers, though, are not the Cardinals. Green Bay enters this game allowing the third-fewest net yards per attempt in the league, which accounts for yardage lost due to sacks in addition to passing yards allowed. The Packers have also yielded only 15 touchdowns through the air while intercepting a league-high 14 passes. Their safeties, in particular, have been ballhawks, with Evan Williams and Xavier McKinney combining for nine of those 14 picks and McKinney leading the league with six on his own.

Green Bay has been just about average in generating pressure so far this season, recording a sack, hit or hurry on 36.2% of opponent dropbacks, according to Tru Media. (The league average is 36.4% this year.) Getting Parsons back in the fold should help tremendously. He led the NFL in total pressures between 2021 and 2025 despite rushing the passer considerably less often than players like Myles Garrett and Maxx Crosby, who are the only players within 30 pressures of Parsons' total during that span. Parsons will likely play limited snaps, but those snaps should be concentrated on important downs and Matthew Stafford and Co. will need to be aware of where he is at all times.

The Packers have also done a pretty strong job of limiting opposing run games, allowing only 4.0 yards per carry. The Rams, though, have sported one of the best rushing attacks in the league, with Williams and Corum combining to average 4.6 yards a pop. The offensive line has cleared the way for 1.79 yards before contact per attempt, which is the second-best mark in the league behind only that of the Bills. Williams and Corum (and Ronnie Rivers) also have a combined 21.0% avoided tackle rate, per Tru Media, which is the seventh-best in football.

The Rams will likely have to lean on their run game here to move the ball, because the situation does not line up well for them through the air. The offensive line's ability to dictate the game at the line of scrimmage will likely determine whether they can achieve any degree of offensive success.

The lift the Packers get from Micah Parsons' return, plus the limitations the Rams' offense should have while working without Nacua, Adams and Ferguson, push me toward Green Bay in this game. I don't foresee the Rams moving the ball as consistently against the Packers as they did against the Cardinals in their first game without all their playmakers. Meanwhile, the Packers have the goods to challenge L.A.'s secondary downfield with Watson and Golden and over the middle with Kraft. Love should be able to get going through the air, even if Green Bay still can't run the ball. Pick: Packers 24, Rams 17
